**Workstreams themselves are drag-and-drop reorderable in the shared sidebar (`renderSidebar()`/`#wsList`) — an explicit user request, mirroring items' own reordering above almost exactly.** Every workstream already had an `order: Number` (assigned at creation, `workstreams.length`, i.e. always the bottom of the list) and `normalizeData()` already re-sorted the array by it on every load — reordering just needed the drag interaction wired up, no new data-model work. `dragStartWs(e, id)`/`dropOnWs(e, targetId)`/`reorderWorkstream(fromId, targetId)` are `draggedItemId`/`dragStartItem`/`dropOnItem`/`reorderItem`'s counterparts, over `draggedWsId` and the flat `workstreams` array directly — there's no per-workstream "same group" guard the way items need `dragged.workstreamId === target.workstreamId`, since workstreams aren't nested inside anything else to stay grouped by; a drop just moves the dragged workstream to the target's position and reassigns clean, contiguous `order` values to the whole array, same "never needs a repair pass" reasoning as `reorderItem()`. The drag handle (`.ws-row-drag`, reusing the shared `.drag-handle` grip-icon class) sits right before `.ws-row-edit`, both hover-revealed the same way (`display: none` at rest, `.ws-row:hover` reveals both) and both gated to Editor+ — matching workstream CRUD's own tier, not a new one. Unlike `.item-actions`'s `visibility` toggle (chosen specifically so a *fixed-width* actions column never reflows the row on hover), `.ws-row-drag`/`.ws-row-edit` use plain `display: none`/`inline-flex`, since neither one reserves dedicated column width to begin with — there's no layout to preserve either way, they're just absent from flow until hovered. The drop target is the whole row (`ondragover`/`ondrop` on `.ws-row` itself), same reasoning as an item row's own: a much bigger, easier target than the grip handle alone. The "All Workstreams" pseudo-row (`filterWorkstreamId === null`) has no `id` and isn't part of `workstreams`, so it never gets a drag handle or drop target at all — nothing to reorder relative to.

**An item's own `actualDate` is intentionally not shown or editable on the row at all** — only in the item modal. `itemRowHtml()`'s `actualDateHtml` is a plain empty `<span></span>` (a placeholder, not an omitted element — the "Actual" grid column is still real and meaningful for the milestone rows below it, just never populated by the item's own row). This was a deliberate simplification after the row briefly showed it as an always-present inline input (mirroring the milestone rows) — the item's own actual-completion-date is enough of an edge-case field, relative to the far-more-common per-milestone actual dates, that it didn't earn a permanent slot in the compact row view.

### IT/Business/Budget tags

Three item-level-only RAG flags — `itStatus`/`businessStatus`/`budgetStatus`, each one of `RAG3` (`['green','amber','red']` — no `not-started`/`complete` concept, unlike the main status). Rendered as icon-only badges (`fa-laptop-code`/`fa-briefcase`/`fa-euro-sign`, colored by the same `--stat-*` variables as the main status badge) in the item row's Tags column; clicking one calls `cycleItemAttr(itemId, field)`, which steps through `RAG3` and wraps — the same pattern as `cycleMilestoneStatus()` but scoped to a plain 3-value cycle instead of the full `STATUSES` list. These are item-only: `milestoneRowsHtml()` never renders them, and `ITEM_TAG_FIELDS` (top of the `<script>` block) is the single list normalizeData() walks to backfill any missing/invalid value back to `'green'`. A brand-new item gets its defaults at creation in `saveItem()`'s `else` branch (not as part of the shared `data` object also used for edits) specifically so re-saving an *existing* item through the modal — which never edits these fields — can't reset them back to green.

**A Journey/Sub Journey's own tags are computed, not hand-set — an explicit user request** ("journeys should roll-up their IT/Business/Budget from Sub-Journeys. Sub-journeys roll this up from their linked scope items... this should not be editable on journey and sub journey level"), the same "no persisted state, always fresh at render time" treatment Status and Plan dates already get for these two types (see "Journeys" below) and for the identical reason — recomputing avoids hooking every place a connected item's own tag could change just to keep a cached value in sync. `computeSubJourneyTagStatus(subJourneyId, field)` is `computeSubJourneyStatus()`'s own counterpart for one RAG3 field: the same weakest-link reduce over `connectedScopeItems(subJourneyId)`'s own tag values — `STATUS_SEVERITY`'s existing red/amber/green ordering already covers `RAG3` outright, so no separate severity table was needed. `computeJourneyTagStatus(journeyId, field)` is one level further removed, reducing over every connected Sub Journey's own already-computed tag value, mirroring `computeJourneyStatus()`'s relationship to `computeSubJourneyStatus()`. Both return `null` when nothing is connected yet; every caller falls back to `'green'` (a Sub Journey with nothing connected also contributes `'green'` into its own parent Journey's reduce, not skipped — the same "empty still participates" reasoning `computeJourneyStatus()`'s own `'not-started'` fallback already uses, just green being the RAG3 equivalent of "nothing to report"). `itemRowHtml()`'s `tagsHtml` reads from these two functions instead of `it[f]` whenever `isJourney`/`isSubJourney`, and renders every one of the three badges as the plain, non-clickable `<span>` regardless of role — `editable` alone isn't enough to gate this (it's `true` for a Planner/Editor on these rows, driving the row's *other* actions), so `isJourney`/`isSubJourney` are checked explicitly on top of it to force the read-only span either way, the same way Status's own `<select>`-vs-badge toggle is forced by item type rather than role. The item's own stored `itStatus`/`businessStatus`/`budgetStatus` fields are left otherwise untouched on a Journey/Sub Journey (`saveItem()` still stamps `'green'` defaults at creation, same as any other item) — they're simply never read back for display any more, the same vestigial-but-harmless treatment the stored `status` field already gets for these two types.

### Dependencies

**This whole feature — the data-model fields, every function/id built on them, and the UI label — was originally named "External Delivery"/`externalDelivery`, renamed to "Dependency"/`dependency` (an explicit user request, to match the newer Dependency category — see "Categories (Admin mode)" above and the updated `Milestone_Lookup_Table.xlsx`).** Every quoted user request below predates the rename and still says "External Delivery"/"External Deliveries" verbatim, since that was genuinely the feature's name when each request was made; the identifiers and prose describing current behavior use the new names throughout.

**A plain boolean flag, `dependency`, marking whether a scope item is a dependency on an external party — an explicit user request — plus a conditional `dependencySpoc` text field for that party's point of contact once the flag is on.** Unlike the IT/Business/Budget tags above, this is a modal-only pair, not a row-level badge: `itemModalBg` gets a plain checkbox (`#itemDependencyInput`) right before the Milestones editor, and a second field (`#itemDependencySpocField`/`#itemDependencySpocInput`) that only *shows* while the checkbox is checked — `onItemDependencyChange()` toggles its `display`, called both from the checkbox's own `onchange` and once at the end of `openItemModal()` so a reopened item starts in the right state. This mirrors the Not Applicable milestone pattern (a field only shown, not just disabled, while conditional on a flag) rather than the IT/Business/Budget tags' own always-visible-badge treatment, since a SPOC name has nowhere sensible to sit in the row's already-full grid the way a single RAG icon does — see "Column layout is a real CSS grid" above for why that grid resists casually adding another column.

`saveItem()` reads the checkbox directly into `dependency`, and reads the SPOC input **only while the checkbox is checked** — `dependencySpoc` is forced back to `null` the moment `dependency` is unchecked and saved, the same "a field that's no longer applicable doesn't keep a stale value" stance Not Applicable milestones take with their own Due/Actual dates (see "Not Applicable milestones" below). Both fields live in the same shared `data` object every other directly-editable field does (unlike `itStatus`/etc. above, which are cycle-only and deliberately excluded from `data`) — a brand-new item and an edit to an existing one both go through the identical read/write path. `openItemModal()`/the `editable` disabling loop treat both new inputs exactly like every other field: seeded from the item (or blank/unchecked for a new one) and `.disabled = !hasRole('editor')`, so the modal stays viewable but not editable below Editor, same as everything else in it. `normalizeData()` backfills `dependency` to `false` for legacy data missing the field, and self-heals `dependencySpoc` back to `null` whenever `dependency` is falsy (or the stored value isn't a string) — the same invariant `saveItem()` enforces on save, just also covering data that arrived from an older export, a hand-edited import, or a linked file predating this feature.

**A workstream's own scope item list separates out its dependency items into their own sub-section, right underneath the main list — an explicit user request** ("show scope items which are flagged as external separate from the scope item list... similar like unassigned scope items... under the scope items list"). `renderStatusView()` splits each workstream's `wItems` into `mainItems`/`dependencyItems` by `it.dependency`; `mainItems` render in the section's normal list exactly as before, and — only if `dependencyItems.length` — a `.ws-subsection-header` ("Dependencies" — pluralized by a later, explicit user request for consistency with the sidebar's own nav entry, see below; a globe icon) plus those items' own rows render right after, still inside the same `.ws-section` card. This mirrors the Unassigned bucket's own "items that don't belong in the normal list" separation, but nested inside a single workstream's own section rather than a whole separate page-wide pinned block — a dependency item still has a real `workstreamId`, this is purely a display grouping. The workstream header's own RAG pill counts are computed from the *full*, unsplit `wItems` beforehand, so a dependency item still counts toward the workstream's overall stats regardless of which half of the list it renders in. The main list's own "No items yet." placeholder only shows when the workstream is genuinely empty (`!mainItems.length && !dependencyItems.length`) — a workstream whose only items are all dependencies shows nothing in the main-list area (the sub-section below already has real content) rather than a misleading "No items yet." above a non-empty dependency list.

**The SPOC briefly also showed directly on the row, then was removed again.** A related explicit user request ("show also the External SPOC in the view") first added it — reusing `itemRowHtml()`'s own existing always-blank grid slot (column 7, "Milestone-Due" in `--item-grid-cols`' own comment — that column only ever holds a milestone's own Due date on a milestone *sub*-row, never anything on the item's own row) rather than adding a whole new grid column for it, rendering `.item-external-spoc` (small, muted text) whenever `it.dependency`, with a "—" placeholder if one hadn't been entered yet. A later, explicit user request ("remove the External Delivery SPOC from the list view") reversed just the row display — `dependencySpocHtml` in `itemRowHtml()` is now unconditionally a bare `<span></span>`, keeping the grid column alive (same "never an omitted cell" discipline every other column in this grid follows — see "Column layout is a real CSS grid" above) without rendering anything into it. The `.item-external-spoc` CSS class was removed outright (it had no other caller left; the class name itself was never renamed, being dead code with nothing left to rename). This only ever touched the row's own display — `dependencySpoc` itself is untouched: still stored, still a real field in the data model, still editable in the item modal's own SPOC input, same as before.

**Dependencies also has a cross-workstream way to see every dependency item at once — but, after several revisions, it is now a Planning quick-filter chip, not a separate page.** The idea started as its own `mode` (`'external'`, an explicit user request, "add External Delivery to the left navigation pane, under 'All Workstreams'"), mirroring how Journeys got its own entry point. Its entry point then moved twice more — first from a sidebar row into `#topNavList`'s ordered group, then out of the sidebar entirely into its own topbar `.btn` (`#tabExternal`, next to `#tabAdmin`) per a later request to "move External Deliveries... to the top navigation bar, detached from... Planning/Dashboard/Review" — and along the way it also gained genuine `filterWorkstreamId` scoping it never used to have (unlike Journeys, which still ignores the selector entirely, a dependency item always belongs to a real workstream, so this narrowing made sense once asked for). A further, separate explicit user request removed the standalone mode/page concept altogether: "add a search pill for 'External Deliveries' under search bar. Remove the 'External Deliveries' button from top navigation bar." **`mode === 'external'`, `MODES`' own entry for it, `#tabExternal`, and `renderExternalDelivery()` are all gone** — replaced by `planningDependencyFilter`, a plain boolean quick-filter chip living in Planning's own toolbar (see "Status board" above, `renderPlanningStatusChips()`), right alongside the status chips and separated from them by a small `.chip-divider`. `matchesPlanningDependencyFilter(it)` (`!planningDependencyFilter || it.dependency`) composes with the search box and the status filter exactly the way `matchesPlanningStatusFilter()` already does — same call sites, same "narrows the list, never the RAG counts, omits an empty section entirely" rules. Unlike the status chips (single-select, one `STATUSES` id at a time), this is a plain on/off toggle (`setPlanningDependencyFilter()`), since `dependency` is itself a boolean field with nothing to select between. Toggling it on still interacts naturally with the *per-workstream* Dependencies sub-section documented just above: with the filter on, every item left on the board is a dependency by definition, so each visible workstream's own main list ends up empty and everything shown sits in that workstream's own sub-section — no special-casing needed, since `mainItems`/`dependencyItems`' existing empty-state logic already handles that split correctly regardless of *why* the main list happens to be empty.

**Finally, the feature itself (chip label, checkbox/field labels, sub-section header, data-model fields, and every function/id) was renamed from "External Delivery"/`externalDelivery` to "Dependency"/`dependency` — an explicit user request, made once the Dependency *category* (see "Categories (Admin mode)" above) existed and the two names sitting side by side read as confusing.** The rename was applied in two passes: first the user-visible labels only (mirroring how Admin mode's tab reads "Settings" while every internal identifier still says `admin` — see "Categories (Admin mode)" above), then, on a later explicit follow-up request ("also clean up the data model for all pending changes"), the underlying data-model fields and every function/element-id built on them too — unlike the Admin/Settings precedent, this time the internal names were brought in line with the new label rather than being left to drift from it. `.item-external-spoc` (dead CSS, see above) and a handful of purely historical comments describing what the feature used to be called/shaped like before this rename are the only surviving "External Delivery" spellings in the codebase.

**Picking the Dependency category now auto-checks the item's own `dependency` flag too — a later, explicit user request** ("if a scope item gets the category Dependency assigned, set the Dependency flag with it") — closing the gap between the two, now-similarly-named but otherwise independent concepts once and for all: previously, choosing the Dependency *category* did nothing to the boolean *flag*, so a scope item could easily end up categorized as a dependency without ever showing up in the Dependencies sub-section/quick-filter chip, which read as a real inconsistency once the two shared a name. `isDependencyCategory(categoryId)` (next to `isPendingCategory()`) is the match.

**The match is a real `dependency: true` flag on the category object itself, not a name comparison — a later, explicit user follow-up ("should I make Dependency a reserved category?") reversing this feature's own first cut, which matched `c.name.trim().toLowerCase() === 'dependency'` directly.** That first version had a real, silent failure mode: renaming the category — even just fixing a typo — would quietly stop the auto-flag from ever firing again, with no error and nothing to notice. `DEFAULT_CATEGORIES`' own "Dependency" entry now carries `dependency: true` directly (threaded through both places that map `DEFAULT_CATEGORIES` into real category objects — `seedDefaults()` and `normalizeData()`'s empty-categories reseed), and `normalizeData()`'s per-category backfill defaults a missing flag to `false` — **except for a category already named "Dependency" from before this flag existed, which backfills to `true` instead, a one-time migration matching the old name-check exactly, so an existing save's already-working auto-flag behavior doesn't silently break the moment this ships.** After that one backfill, the name is never consulted again — only the flag is.

**This is deliberately *not* the same treatment Pending gets, despite both now being boolean-flagged categories** — an explicit design choice weighed against making Dependency a fully reserved category the way Pending is (undeletable, excluded from the ordinary item modal's Category picker and the scope-assign modal's choices, locked Workstream/Category fields on any item using it). None of those restrictions fit Dependency's actual use case, which is meant to stay a completely ordinary, freely-selectable, freely-deletable, freely-renamable category in every other respect — the flag exists solely so *this one auto-nudge* survives a rename, not to reserve the category itself. Concretely: `saveCategory()`'s edit path only ever reassigns `c.name`/`c.milestones`, never touching `c.dependency`, so renaming (or re-templating) the category through the ordinary category modal preserves the flag for free, with no special-case code needed there at all; its new-category path never sets the flag at all, since only the one originally-seeded category (or one migrated from a pre-existing "Dependency"-named save, per above) is ever meant to carry it — there's no UI to hand-toggle it onto an arbitrary category, mirroring how `pending: true` has no toggle either. Deleting the flagged category is allowed (unlike Pending) and simply turns the auto-flag feature off going forward — nothing reseeds it, since (unlike Pending) nothing else in the app actually depends on this category continuing to exist. The Admin category list's "Reserved" badge (`renderAdmin()`'s per-row `pendingBadge`) deliberately isn't extended to a `dependency: true` category either — showing one would misleadingly imply the same undeletable/picker-excluded protections Pending's badge is actually warning about, none of which apply here.

Two call sites, the only two places a real (non-Pending, non-Journey) item's `categoryId` is ever actually assigned:
- `onItemCategoryChange()` — the item modal's Category `<select>` `onchange`. Deliberately placed *ahead of* that function's existing `if (editingItemId) return` early return (see "Milestone checklist" just below), not behind it: re-seeding `editingMilestones` from a new category is destructive to in-progress customization, which is exactly why that reseed is restricted to a brand-new item only — but checking a checkbox loses nothing, so the auto-flag applies whether the modal is creating a new item or editing an already-saved one. It checks `#itemDependencyInput` and calls `onItemDependencyChange()` (revealing the SPOC field, the same as a manual click would).
- `applyScopeCategory()` — the Pending-item scope-assign flow (see "Quick-add scope items..." below), which sets a real `categoryId` directly rather than through the modal's own checkbox at all. Sets `it.dependency = true` directly when the newly-assigned category is Dependency; a still-Pending item always starts with `dependency: false` (the field is hidden in the modal for one), so there's no existing `true` value this could ever clobber back to `false`.

**One-directional, like every other automatic flag-nudge in this app (Pending-status auto-advance, Not Applicable preserving rather than resetting `status`)**: picking Dependency turns the flag *on*; switching to a *different* category afterward never turns it back off. This is deliberate, not an oversight — the flag might have been checked by hand for a reason unrelated to category (or checked automatically by an earlier Dependency pick that's since been reconsidered), and silently discarding that on an unrelated category change would be a worse surprise than leaving a stale-looking checked box the user can still uncheck by hand at any time. `saveItem()` needed no changes at all for this — it already reads `#itemDependencyInput`'s live checked state into `dependency` on Save (see the "plain boolean flag" paragraph above), including on the destructive category-switch confirm-modal path (`swapData = Object.assign({}, data, {...})` inherits `data.dependency` unchanged), so the checkbox having already been auto-checked by `onItemCategoryChange()` before Save was even clicked is enough on its own.

### Milestone checklist

A new item's milestone checklist is seeded from whichever **Category** is selected in the item modal (see "Categories (Admin mode)" below) — not a single fixed list. `milestonesForCategory(categoryId, dueDate)` builds a fresh `{id, name, dueDate, status}` array from `categoryById(categoryId).milestones` (plain name strings), each given its own freshly-generated `id`, `status: 'pending'`, and `dueDate: null` — no longer dated to the given `dueDate` argument at all; see "Pending status" above for why. `openItemModal(id)` seeds `editingMilestones` — a module-level working copy, not committed to `items` until Save — from `milestonesForCategory()` when creating a new item, or from a deep-ish copy of the existing item's `milestones` when editing one already saved. This is why editing never resets an item back to its category's template: `saveItem()` only ever writes whatever is currently in `editingMilestones`, regardless of how it originally got there.

The Category `<select>`'s `onchange` calls `onItemCategoryChange()`, which re-seeds `editingMilestones` from the newly-picked category — but only `if (!editingItemId)`. Switching category while *creating* an item is expected to reload the checklist to match (you haven't customized anything yet); switching it while *editing* an already-saved item must never silently discard whatever checklist customization is already in `editingMilestones` mid-edit — `tests/cases/categories.test.js`'s "does nothing while editing an existing item" test guards this exact distinction. That doesn't mean switching category on an already-saved item does nothing at all, though — see "Switching an existing item's category" just below for what `saveItem()` itself does with it. `DEFAULT_CATEGORIES` (top of the `<script>` block — an array of `{name, milestones}` plus, on the Dependency entry only, `dependency: true` (see "Dependencies" above) — one entry per out-of-the-box category: Development, Artefact, Activity, Dependency, content taken from `Milestone_Lookup_Table.xlsx`) is *not* a live default any more — it's only read once, by `normalizeData()`/`seedDefaults()`, to seed the categories a fresh install (or a pre-categories save) starts with, alongside the separately-seeded, reserved Pending category (see "Quick-add scope items, the Unassigned bucket, and the Pending category" below). `DEFAULT_CATEGORY_MILESTONES` is kept only as an alias for `DEFAULT_CATEGORIES[0].milestones` (Development's own template), for the handful of call sites/tests that only ever cared about "the first/default category's milestones" from back when Development was the only one seeded.

`renderMilestonesEditor()` redraws the modal's milestone rows from `editingMilestones`; each row's `name`/`dueDate`/`status` field writes straight back into `editingMilestones[idx]` via inline `oninput`/`onchange` (no re-render on keystroke, so inputs never lose focus mid-edit) — `saveItem()` reads the array directly rather than re-scraping the DOM. `removeMilestoneRow(idx)` is the only mutation that re-renders the editor, since that one's structural (row count changes, so every subsequent row's index shifts). None of this touches `items` until Save is clicked — Cancel (or closing the modal) just discards `editingMilestones`, which `closeItemModal()` also resets to `[]` for hygiene. **There used to be a matching `addMilestoneRow()`/"+ Add milestone" button here too, letting an existing item's checklist grow beyond its category's template — removed outright (an explicit user request), not hidden: the button, the function, and its test coverage are all gone.** A milestone can still be renamed, reordered, removed, or marked Not Applicable, and a brand-new item's checklist can still be reseeded by switching category *while still creating it* (`onItemCategoryChange()`) — but once an item is saved, its set of milestones is fixed at whatever the category template gave it minus whatever's been removed; there's no way back into the modal to hand-add one more. `updateItemStatusFieldMode()`'s own comment (below) previously cited `addMilestoneRow()` as the reason `#itemDueInput` stays kept in sync even while hidden — that's now `onItemCategoryChange()`'s own read of the same field, still a real, current reason, just a different call site.

### Switching an existing item's category

**Picking a different Category for an item that's already saved — and already has real milestones — is destructive, and confirm-gated, unlike every other field this modal edits.** An explicit user request ("if I change the category of a scope item which had already a category with its respective milestones... deleting milestones and assign new milestone skeleton according to new category"). This is a *reaction to* `saveItem()`, not `onItemCategoryChange()` — that function still no-ops for an existing item exactly as documented above, so browsing the dropdown mid-edit does nothing on its own; only actually clicking Save with a genuinely different category selected triggers anything, mirroring `saveCategory()`'s own "close the editor, defer the destructive part to a confirm modal" pattern (see "Category template edits sync to items already using that category" below) rather than reacting live to every keystroke/selection change.

`saveItem()` checks this before committing anything: if the item being edited (not a Journey, not still Pending — see below for both) currently has `editingItemForSave.categoryId !== categoryId` (the newly-picked one) **and** `editingItemForSave.milestones.length` is non-zero, it doesn't save at all yet. It closes the item modal and opens the confirm modal instead — `confirmLabel: 'Switch Category'`, body describing the impact in plain terms ("Switching to '<new category>' will replace this item's N milestone(s) with '<new category>'s own template (losing any status/dates recorded on them). This cannot be undone.") — with the actual swap deferred into the confirm modal's `action`, exactly like `saveCategory()`'s own destructive-diff confirm. The swap itself mirrors `applyScopeCategory()` (Pending's own scope-assign flow) precisely: every one of the item's existing milestones is tombstoned (`deletedMilestoneIds`, so a merge can't resurrect them from a stale external copy), a fresh checklist is built via `milestonesForCategory(categoryId, editingItemForSave.dueDate)`, and `status`/plan dates are recomputed from that fresh (all pending) template the same way `computedStatusFromMilestones()`/`computedDateRangeFromMilestones()` always drive them. This is a **full swap, not a name-diffed merge** the way `applyCategoryMilestoneSync()`'s own same-category template-sync is — two different categories' milestone names are essentially disjoint (Development's "Requirements Defined" has no equivalent in Artefact's own template), so there's nothing meaningful to preserve by matching names across the switch. `logReviewChange()` records the switch into whichever review cycle is currently active for the item's workstream, the same "what changed while this workstream was under review" trail `applyScopeCategory()` already leaves behind — a no-op if none is active. Cancelling the confirm modal discards the whole edit (name, dates, tags — everything else typed into the item modal too), same as `saveCategory()`'s own cancel: by the time the confirm modal is showing, the item modal is already closed, so there's no "back" state to return the user to.

**An item with *zero* current milestones has nothing to lose, so it silently picks up the new category's template instead — no confirm needed.** There would otherwise be no way at all to get milestones onto it under the new category: the "+ Add milestone" button was removed for an existing item's checklist (see above), and `onItemCategoryChange()` deliberately no-ops once an item exists — without this silent-reseed case, a real (non-Pending) item that somehow ended up with zero milestones would be permanently stuck with none, regardless of which category it was switched to. This is computed earlier in `saveItem()`, at the same point the modal's own milestone edits are normally read (`editingMilestones.map(...)`) — `zeroMilestoneCategorySwitch` swaps in `milestonesForCategory(categoryId, editingItemForSave.dueDate)` right there instead, so the rest of the function (status/date-range computation, `stampChangedMilestones()`'s own diff against the target's — empty — prior milestones) treats it exactly like any other fresh set of milestones with nothing to reconcile against.

**This never applies to a Journey** (forced `categoryId: null` regardless — see "Journeys" below, a Journey has no category at all) **or a still-Pending item** (`stillPendingOnSave` already forces `categoryId` to stay whatever it was — see "Quick-add scope items..." below; the only door out of Pending is `applyScopeCategory()`, not this). Both guards are the same ones the rest of `saveItem()`'s category handling already uses, just extended to gate this new destructive path too.

**The reserved Pending category is now also excluded from the ordinary item modal's Category `<select>` entirely** (`populateCategorySelect()`), a related fix uncovered while building this: without it, a real item could be manually switched *into* Pending through this ordinary dropdown — bypassing the entire point of Pending being reachable only via `applyScopeCategory()` — and would land in a broken mixed state (a real `workstreamId`, but a Pending `categoryId` whose own lock then permanently disables both the Workstream and Category selects on it, with no way back out through the normal edit flow). The one exception is `selectedId` itself already being Pending: `categories.filter(c => !c.pending || c.id === selectedId)` still includes it so a genuinely still-Pending item being edited renders "Pending" as its own selected option (matching its real state) rather than silently falling back to whichever real category happens to render first — that select is locked/disabled for such an item regardless (see "A still-Pending item's category can never be changed..." below), so this is purely about what's *displayed*, not what's *reachable*.

**Delayed-milestone color coding on the status board's Due/Actual pills** (`milestoneRowsHtml()`, an explicit user request): this is a purely visual signal layered on top of a milestone's own hand-set `status` (RAG) field, not a replacement for it — a milestone can be "On Track" and still show a red Due pill if its target date has quietly slipped by. Two independent flags:
- `dueOverdue` — `!m.notApplicable && m.status !== 'complete' && m.dueDate && m.dueDate < todayStr()`, the exact same "overdue" definition the dashboard already uses for whole items (`renderDashboard()`'s `overdue` filter) — a milestone still open past its own Due date. Reads on the Due pill only; a Complete milestone is never flagged here regardless of how late it finished (see the next flag for that).
- `actualLate` — `!m.notApplicable && m.actualDate && m.dueDate && m.actualDate > m.dueDate && m.actualDate <= todayStr()` — the Actual date itself was recorded after the Due date, whether or not `status` was ever marked Complete. Reads on the Actual pill only. The `<= todayStr()` half is an explicit user request, added after the first cut flagged *any* Actual later than Due, including one still in the future: an Actual date is sometimes logged ahead of time (a planned completion date), and a plan that hasn't happened yet isn't a late finish — red is reserved for one that's already occurred. `<=`, not `<`, since an Actual date of today has by definition already happened (unlike `dueOverdue`'s own `<` on Due, where today's target hasn't necessarily passed yet).
  Both use the same red: `.date-pill.pill-overdue` (background/text recolored to `--stat-red`/`--stat-red-bg`, same palette as `.attention-tag.overdue` on the dashboard). `dateInputHtml(value, attrs, pillClass)` grew a third, optional parameter for this — every other call site (item Start/Due, the Minutes/Actions modal, a review-history before/after pair) just omits it and gets the plain pill, unaffected. During a review (`reviewCycle` passed in), Due renders as a read-only `<span class="item-dates-computed">` rather than a pill (see "Review mode" below) — `dueOverdue` still applies there too, just as a plain inline `color:var(--stat-red)` on that span instead of the `pill-overdue` class, since there's no pill to attach it to. A `notApplicable` milestone is excluded from both flags outright, matching every other "notApplicable freezes/ignores this field" convention on this row (see the Status badge and Due-cell comments just above).

### Item status roll-up

An item's `status` is **computed, not hand-set, whenever it has at least one milestone** — `computedStatusFromMilestones(milestones)` picks whichever milestone ranks worst by `STATUS_SEVERITY` (`{red:0, amber:1, green:2, 'not-started':3, complete:4}`, lower = worse/weaker) and that becomes the item's status. This is a deliberate reversal of an earlier design (previously documented here as "no roll-up, status is always manual") — the product call was that a checklist item at Red should always be visible at the parent level, not silently masked by a manual status someone forgot to update.

The severity order is *not* the same as `STATUSES`' display order: `not-started` ranks near the *best* end (better than Green, worse only than Complete) rather than at the start — the reasoning is that "hasn't started yet" needs no attention the way an active Amber/Red does, so it shouldn't drag an otherwise-on-track item's rollup down the way a genuinely active problem should. Only Green/Amber/Red (milestones someone is actively tracking) can make an item look worse than "nothing to report yet."

**An item with zero milestones keeps a manual status** — there's nothing to compute from, so `computedStatusFromMilestones([])` returns `null` and callers fall back to whatever's hand-picked. Three places enforce/apply the rule, all calling the same helper so the logic lives in exactly one spot:
- `saveItem()`: `milestones.length ? computedStatusFromMilestones(milestones) : <Status select's value>`.
- `cycleMilestoneStatus()`: recomputes `it.status` immediately after changing one milestone's status, so the item row's badge is never stale even without reopening the modal.
- `normalizeData()`: recomputes `it.status` for every item with milestones on every load/merge/import — this is what self-heals a manual status saved before this rule existed, or one arriving from an external file that predates/ignores it, and is the actual source of truth the other two are just keeping in sync ahead of the next `normalizeData()` pass.

**The item modal's Status field switches between an editable `<select>` and a read-only computed badge**, live, based on whether the item currently has milestones — never both at once. `updateItemStatusFieldMode()` (name predates the date-range roll-up below, which it now also drives — see there for why it wasn't split into two functions) toggles `#itemStatusSelect`/`#itemStatusComputed`'s `display` and, in the computed case, fills `#itemStatusComputedBadge` from `computedStatusFromMilestones(editingMilestones)` (the in-progress working copy, not yet saved). It's called from `renderMilestonesEditor()` (covers modal-open, add-row, remove-row — anything that changes the milestone *count*) and separately from each milestone row's status `<select>` `onchange` (a status *change* without a count change, which wouldn't otherwise trigger a re-render). Since it only ever reads `editingMilestones` and writes a few small DOM nodes, it's cheap enough to call defensively from several places rather than trying to track more precisely which one actually changed.

### Item plan date range roll-up

An item's plan date range (`startDate`/`dueDate`) is **computed from its milestones whenever it has at least one**, the same "reversal of an earlier manual-only design" as status above and for the same reason — a milestone that slipped shouldn't be silently invisible at the parent level because nobody went back and adjusted the item's own dates by hand. `computedDateRangeFromMilestones(milestones)` returns `{startDate, dueDate}` spanning the earliest to the latest date across *every* milestone's `dueDate` **and, when set, its `actualDate`** — considering actual dates too means a milestone that actually finished earlier or later than planned widens the item's range to match reality, not just what was originally scheduled. Returns `null` for an empty list, same convention as `computedStatusFromMilestones`.

**An item with zero milestones keeps a manual date range** — same fallback pattern as status. The same three call sites keep it in sync, plus one more that status doesn't need:
- `saveItem()`: `milestones.length ? computedDateRangeFromMilestones(milestones) : <manual Start/Due inputs>`.
- `updateMilestoneDateField()`: recomputes `it.startDate`/`it.dueDate` immediately after a milestone's due or actual date changes inline on the status board — a milestone's date is exactly the kind of edit that can happen without ever opening the item modal (see "Status board" above), so this can't wait for `saveItem()`.
- `applyCategoryMilestoneSync()`: a category sync can add or remove milestones on an item without the item modal ever opening, so it recomputes the range (alongside status) right after mutating `it.milestones`.
- `normalizeData()`: recomputes on every load/merge/import, self-healing legacy/stale values — the actual source-of-truth pass the other three are just keeping ahead of.

**Both the item modal and the status-board row hide manual date editing once milestones exist**, mirroring the Status field exactly: `#itemDatesManual` (the Start/Due `<input>` pair) and `#itemDatesComputed` (a read-only badge + explanatory note) toggle together with `#itemStatusSelect`/`#itemStatusComputed` inside the same `updateItemStatusFieldMode()` — which is why that function wasn't split in two despite the name; Status and Plan-dates always change computed-vs-manual mode in lockstep, driven by the same "does this item have milestones" check. The function also keeps the (possibly hidden) `#itemStartInput`/`#itemDueInput` values in sync with the live-computed range, since `onItemCategoryChange()` still reads `#itemDueInput`'s value when re-seeding a still-being-created item's milestones from a newly-picked category. On the status board itself, `itemRowHtml()`'s `planDatesHtml` renders `.item-dates-computed` (plain text, a tooltip explains it's derived) instead of the editable `.item-dates-inline` pair whenever `it.milestones.length > 0` — the item's own separate `actualDate` field (see "Data model" above — a distinct concept from the plan range) stays independently editable on the row regardless, since it was never part of what's being computed here.

### Not Applicable milestones

**A milestone can be marked Not Applicable — an explicit user request ("in planning view, allow setting a milestone to not applicable") — via a standalone `notApplicable: Boolean` field, independent of `status`, not a 6th value added to `STATUSES`/the status click-cycle.** Two interaction models were considered and discussed with the user up front: extending the existing status cycle (reusing the exact click-to-cycle pattern already used everywhere, but adding an occasional-use value to a rotation optimized for the common 5), versus a separate toggle that leaves that cycle completely untouched. The user picked the separate toggle, specifically to avoid diluting the fast, frequent status-cycling interaction with a 6th stop most clicks would never want. Keeping it a boolean rather than a status value also means the milestone's own `status` is *preserved*, not reset, while marked Not Applicable — `toggleMilestoneNotApplicable()`/`toggleEditingMilestoneNotApplicable()` never touch it — so un-marking restores whatever it was before, rather than snapping back to "Not Started".

**Excluded from every roll-up a milestone would otherwise feed into** — the same "never counts toward or against anything" treatment applied everywhere it's relevant:
- `computedStatusFromMilestones(milestones)` filters `notApplicable` milestones out of the weakest-link reduce entirely. If literally every milestone on the item is marked Not Applicable, there's nothing left to reduce over, but the item still *has* milestones (so its Status field must stay in computed mode, never reverting to manual) — `'not-started'` is the fallback there, same neutral value a brand-new milestone already starts at. A genuinely empty list (no milestones at all) still returns `null`, unchanged from before this existed.
- `computedDateRangeFromMilestones(milestones)` excludes a Not Applicable milestone's `dueDate`/`actualDate` from the earliest/latest scan — its date shouldn't stretch the item's displayed plan range for something that was never really going to happen. Returns `null` if every milestone is excluded (same as an empty list); callers (`updateMilestoneDateField()`, `saveItem()`, `applyCategoryMilestoneSync()`, `normalizeData()`) already treat a `null` range as "leave the existing dates alone", so this needed no changes at any of those call sites.
- `itemRowHtml()`'s "X/Y milestones" badge counts only applicable milestones on **both** sides of the fraction (`applicableMilestones = it.milestones.filter(m => !m.notApplicable)`) — counting a Not Applicable milestone in the denominator would make 100% permanently unreachable, and counting it as done would overstate real progress. The chevron/expand toggle and `total` (used for that, and for whether Plan-dates render as computed at all) deliberately stay keyed off the *real*, unfiltered milestone count — an item whose milestones are all Not Applicable must still be expandable, so any of them can be reached and un-marked; the badge just renders empty in that edge case, same as having zero milestones at all.
- `renderDashboard()`'s `milestones` (feeding completion %, the per-workstream summary rows, and the Overdue/Upcoming feeds) is filtered to exclude `notApplicable` right where it's first assembled from `allMilestonesFlat()`, so every downstream calculation inherits the exclusion for free rather than needing its own filter.
- `isItemConfirmedInCycle(cycle, it)` filters to `applicable = it.milestones.filter(m => !m.notApplicable)` before checking "every milestone confirmed" — a Not Applicable milestone can never be the thing blocking an item from reading as confirmed. If `applicable` is empty (every milestone Not Applicable) `applicable.every(...)` is vacuously `true` — confirmed by default, matching "nothing left here that needs reviewing" — and deliberately does **not** fall through to the item's own direct `cycle.confirmations` the zero-milestone case below it uses, since nothing ever writes an entry there for an item that still has real milestones (see `itemRowHtml()`'s `reviewCellHtml`, which always wires an all-N/A item's button to `toggleConfirmAllMilestones()`, never `toggleReviewConfirm()`). `toggleConfirmAllMilestones()` mirrors this: it operates only on `applicable`, and is a no-op (not a 0-length push/splice) when `applicable` is empty. `milestoneRowsHtml()`'s own per-milestone confirm cell renders an inert `fa-ban` placeholder instead of a real `.review-confirm-toggle` for a Not Applicable milestone during a review — not omitted (same column-collapse reason as everywhere else), just never a toggle, since there's nothing to confirm.

**UI, in both the places the user asked for** (status board row + item modal's milestone editor):
- `milestoneRowsHtml()` places the toggle immediately *after* the Status badge — the order was swapped (N/A before Status) and then swapped back again, both times per user request, so this is back to the original Status-then-N/A order — inside one shared `.milestone-status-cell`, a CSS Grid spanning **both** of the row's trailing tracks (`grid-column:9/11` — the Status track (90px at the time, later widened to 116px for "Completed Late" — see "RAG status" above) plus the Actions track at 56px) rather than each control claiming a whole track of its own. That combined cell followed a separate user report ("the n/a column is to broad... make it as small as possible"): giving the icon-only toggle its own full Status-width track (sized for Status's text label, e.g. "Off Track") left it visibly stretched/floating in mostly-empty space no matter how it was aligned *within* that track, since the track's own width doesn't shrink to content — a `justify-self` tweak was tried first and rejected for exactly that reason (it only repositions content inside a still-wide box, it can't shrink the box). The cell's own internal grid (`grid-template-columns: 92px auto` at the time, later widened to `120px auto` — see below) sizes the toggle tight against its fixed-width first column instead, so it stays put regardless of which one comes first — that ordering question and the "make the track fit the content" question turned out to be independent, which is why swapping the order back again didn't need to touch the sizing fix at all. `milestoneHeaderHtml()`'s own "Status"/"MS Req." labels (the header's text for the Not Applicable toggle's column was renamed from "N/A" to "MS Req." — a later, purely cosmetic user request; the toggle itself, its function name, and the frozen "N/A" status-badge text it produces are all unchanged) share the identical `grid-column:9/11` cell, for the same reason a header label and the data beneath it losing sync would misalign the same way an omitted cell would (see "Column layout is a real CSS grid" above) — and neither of these spanning cells touches `itemRowHtml()`, which still separately auto-places its own Status(9)/Actions(10) content untouched, so nothing here affects the item row's own column widths. (These grid-column numbers shifted down by one — from `10/12` and `9`/`10` respectively — when the grid's old "Actual" placeholder column was removed outright per a later user request; see "Column layout is a real CSS grid" above.) The toggle always renders an icon (`fa-regular fa-toggle-off` off, `fa-solid fa-toggle-on` on, colored `var(--stat-not-started)` when on) rather than being omitted when off — the same "always a real, visible icon, just not clickable below the gating role" convention `.review-confirm-toggle` already uses — clickable only at Editor+ (`toggleMilestoneNotApplicable(itemId, milestoneId)`, `requireRole('editor')`-guarded, direct-to-data + immediate `save()`/`render()`, same shape as `cycleMilestoneStatus()`). `renderMilestonesEditor()`'s own row in the item modal mirrors the same order (Status then N/A, next to Remove) for consistency between the two places this toggle lives.

**The icon itself was `fa-ban` originally, swapped to `fa-toggle-off`/`fa-toggle-on` on a later, explicit user request.** The request was open-ended ("suggest other icons for the N/A toggle, show these suggestions to me") — six candidates (`fa-ban`, `fa-circle-minus`, `fa-toggle-off`/`fa-toggle-on`, `fa-slash`, `fa-square-minus`, `fa-circle-xmark`) were rendered directly in the real row (both the applicable/off and Not Applicable/on states, side by side) via a live DOM patch in a running browser rather than a static mockup, so what was being compared was pixel-accurate to how it'd actually look; the toggle-switch pair was picked and shipped. This is the one toggle in the whole app whose on/off states use two genuinely different icon *names* rather than the usual style-variant-of-the-same-icon pattern every other toggle here uses (the priority flag, the review confirm toggle) — deliberately: a real toggle-switch glyph changing *shape* between states (not just fill) reads as "on/off" more immediately than a subtler outline-vs-filled distinction alone. Both render sites (`milestoneRowsHtml()`'s board row and `renderMilestonesEditor()`'s modal row) were updated together, same as any other change to this toggle.

**Both states render `fa-solid`, not the `fa-regular`/`fa-solid` pairing every other toggle in this app uses — a user-reported bug found immediately after the icon-name change above shipped** ("still see the old icon... now I have a mix of old and new icon"), traced to Font Awesome's free tier shipping **no `fa-regular` style for either toggle icon at all**, solid-only — `fa-regular fa-toggle-off` silently rendered as the browser's own missing-glyph fallback box instead of an outline toggle, which from a glance looked enough like "the old icon is still there" to read as the fix not having landed at all. (A live DOM-patched mockup used to pick the icon in the first place happened not to catch this, since it swapped classes on an already-loaded page rather than exercising a genuinely fresh render — see the candidate-comparison methodology just above.) Fixed by rendering both states `fa-solid` and letting the icon *name* (`fa-toggle-off` vs `fa-toggle-on`) carry the on/off distinction entirely, same as it always did — the off state's existing `color`/`opacity` styling (muted `--text-faint`/`.35`) is untouched and still what visually separates it from the colored, full-strength on state, just no longer compounded with a broken outline variant. A second, related "mix of old and new icon" report (Review mode's Confirm-column placeholder for a `notApplicable` milestone, independently coded from this toggle and still `fa-ban` at the time) was a genuinely separate bug, fixed alongside this — see `reviewCellHtml` in "Review mode" below.

**Which state maps to which glyph was inverted on a final, explicit user request, after the icon-name and missing-glyph bugs above were both already fixed.** The original mapping (picked when the candidates were first compared, see above) had `notApplicable` render `fa-toggle-on` and the ordinary applicable case render `fa-toggle-off` — reading the toggle as "is this marked Not Applicable." The shipped mapping is the other way around: applicable (the normal case) renders `fa-toggle-on`, `notApplicable` renders `fa-toggle-off` — reading the toggle as "MS Req." itself, matching the column's own header more literally ("is this milestone required" — on for yes, off once marked Not Applicable). The `color`/`opacity` styling that visually distinguishes the two states is untouched by this — it's still keyed off the semantic `m.notApplicable`/`na` boolean directly (colored + full-strength when genuinely Not Applicable, muted otherwise), not off which icon shape happens to be showing, so inverting the icon mapping alone was a one-line ternary swap in each of the three places this icon appears: `milestoneRowsHtml()`'s `naHtml`, `renderMilestonesEditor()`'s `naToggleHtml`, and `reviewCellHtml`'s own inert Confirm-column placeholder (kept in sync with the toggle's own `notApplicable`-state icon, per the "mix of old and new icon" fix above).

**A third `fa-ban` usage was missed on the first pass — a user-reported "mix of old and new icon" that turned out to be a real inconsistency, not a caching issue.** `milestoneRowsHtml()`'s Confirm-column cell (`reviewCellHtml`, `grid-column:12`, shown only when `reviewCycle` is passed — i.e. only in Review mode) renders its own inert "excluded from this review" placeholder for a `notApplicable` milestone, independent of the MS Req. toggle sitting in the column right next to it — the two were never the same code path (see "Confirmation is milestone-level" in "Review mode" below), so updating the toggle's own icon didn't touch this one. In Review mode, both cells render for the same `notApplicable` milestone on the same row — with the toggle now `fa-toggle-on` and this placeholder still `fa-ban`, a reviewer saw two different "not applicable" glyphs side by side, which read exactly like a stale/half-applied icon change even though the underlying code was already fully shipped and correct everywhere else. Fixed by updating this placeholder to the identical `fa-solid fa-toggle-on` (still at its own reduced `opacity:.4`, since it's meant to read as inert/excluded, not an actively lit toggle) — the two cells now agree, since they represent the same underlying concept even though they're driven by separate code.
- While on: the status badge freezes to a plain grey "N/A" (not whatever RAG color `m.status` still holds underneath — showing e.g. "Off Track" and "Not Applicable" at once would read as contradictory rather than "this one's on hold") and is no longer clickable/cycleable; the milestone name mutes (`opacity:.55`); Due and Actual date **values are cleared outright** — a second explicit user request ("if i switch a task to n/a, remove due date and actual date"). `toggleMilestoneNotApplicable()` sets `m.dueDate = null; m.actualDate = null` the moment it's turned on; un-marking does **not** try to restore whatever they were — they stay blank, so a milestone brought back into scope gets a deliberate fresh date rather than an easy-to-miss stale one. This is one of only two fields Not Applicable actually mutates (`status` is the other kind of field — deliberately preserved, not touched, per above); everything else about the milestone (name, id) is untouched.
- **`milestoneRowsHtml()`'s Due cell collapses to a genuinely empty element (not a disabled-but-still-visible pill) whenever `m.notApplicable`** — a third user report, pointing out the inconsistency directly against Actual: a disabled icon+`"tt.mm.jjjj"`-placeholder pill still *read* as a real, if inert, field for a value that's deliberately gone, unlike Actual, which already collapses to nothing once it has nothing to show (the ghost "+" only appears when *editable and empty* — see below). Due now takes the same branch structure Actual's own emptiness check does, just unconditional on `m.notApplicable` rather than on the date being merely absent — because normally toggling *always* clears `dueDate` to `null` in the same breath, the two conditions coincide for anything actually toggled through the UI. They can still diverge for a milestone whose `notApplicable`/dates were set directly (an import, a hand-edited file) rather than through the toggle — Due hides unconditionally regardless of what `dueDate` happens to hold, while Actual only hides when it's genuinely empty, so a leftover non-null `actualDate` on such a record still renders as a disabled pill. This wasn't requested to be equally strict, so it wasn't changed to match.
- Clearing `dueDate` needed two follow-on fixes, since a milestone's `dueDate` had never been nullable before this: `normalizeData()`'s `if (!m.dueDate) m.dueDate = it.dueDate` backfill (meant for a genuinely missing/legacy value) would otherwise silently refill a deliberately-cleared one on the very next load — now gated on `&& !m.notApplicable`. `saveItem()`'s milestone mapping (`dueDate: m.dueDate || manualDueDate`, meant so an *applicable* milestone never ends up dateless) would do the same thing on Save via the item's own manual Due input — now conditioned on `m.notApplicable` to skip that fallback entirely for a Not Applicable row.
- The item modal's `renderMilestonesEditor()` mirrors all of this on the detached `editingMilestones` working copy (not committed until Save): the status `<select>` swaps for the same frozen "N/A" label while on, Due/Actual disable, and the toggle button (`toggleEditingMilestoneNotApplicable(idx)`) sits right after the status field, before Remove — matching the status board's own Status-then-N/A order. `toggleEditingMilestoneNotApplicable()` clears `dueDate`/`actualDate` the same way `toggleMilestoneNotApplicable()` does. Toggling triggers a full `renderMilestonesEditor()` re-render (unlike a plain name/date/status edit, which just writes the value and calls `updateItemStatusFieldMode()`) since the row's own markup shape changes here — the `<select>` becomes a `<span>` or back.
- **`.milestone-status-cell` is a CSS Grid, `grid-template-columns: 120px auto` (originally `92px auto` — widened for "Completed Late", see "RAG status" above), not a flex box** — a fixed-width first column is what keeps the N/A toggle sitting at the same x position regardless of which status label is showing, a user-reported alignment complaint (without a fixed width, a flex/grid child sizes to its own text, so the toggle would sit at a different x position row to row). An earlier version put that same width as a `min-width` on the first *child* itself (the `.status-badge` pill, plus `text-align:center`/`white-space:nowrap`) rather than on the column — which fixed the N/A alignment, but stretched the milestone pill itself to fill it, making it visibly bigger than the item row's own (unconstrained, content-sized) status pill — a second, later user-reported complaint ("status pills for scope item and milestone have different sizes"). Reserving the width on the *grid column* instead (`.milestone-status-cell > :first-child { justify-self: start }`, no forced width on the child) keeps every status pill the same size everywhere it appears, just positioned within its reserved slot — which also means `milestoneHeaderHtml()`'s "Status" label sits in the exact same slot the pill below it does, so the header and the data stay visually locked together horizontally, not just column-aligned. `justify-self` here was briefly `center` (a matching user request to center Due/Actual/Status/N/A headers over their columns), then reverted back to `start` along with Due/Actual's own header alignment once the user asked for every header in this table to go back to left-aligned — the two values were swapped in lockstep both times, since the header and data row share this one rule by design (see the comment on the rule itself for why they can't be split apart without reintroducing the original misalignment bug).
- Both toggles are logged to the active review cycle's change log the same way a status change is (`logReviewChange()`), just as plain text ("Marked as Not Applicable" / "Marked as Applicable again") rather than a structured `statusChange`/`tagChange`/`dateChange` — `reviewHistoryRowHtml()` already falls back to plain text for any entry without one of those three shapes, so nothing there needed to change.

`milestonesForCategory()` (seeding a new item's checklist from its category template), `applyCategoryMilestoneSync()` (adding milestones on template sync), and `openItemModal()`'s existing-item seeding path all construct fresh milestone objects with `notApplicable: false` explicitly — every place in this file that builds a milestone object needed this field added, not just the toggle functions themselves.

### Modals

Eight modals share the `.modal-bg` pattern (add `.open` class to show, remove to hide): `wsModalBg` (create/edit a workstream, with a delete button that moves that workstream's items to Unassigned rather than deleting them — see "Deletion tombstones and undo" below), `itemModalBg` (create/edit a scope item, `.modal-wide` at 520px to fit the milestones editor — see "Milestone checklist" above), `categoryModalBg` (create/edit an Admin-mode category and its milestone template — see "Categories (Admin mode)" below), `confirmModalBg` (generic confirm-then-run-a-callback, used by every delete, by restore-from-backup, and by cancelling a review cycle — see "Deletion tombstones and undo" below), `importModalBg` (merge vs. replace choice after picking a file), `backupsModalBg` (list + restore local daily snapshots), `fileSyncModalBg` (onboarding/reconnect for the linked-file feature), and `backupFolderModalBg` (the second step of that same launch-time onboarding sequence — see "Launch-time onboarding" under "Daily backups to a linked folder" below). All eight close on Escape and on a background click (listeners near the end of the `<script>` block check `e.target.id`) — the one exception elsewhere in this file is `scopeAssignModalBg`, deliberately excluded from both, since it has no dismiss path at all (see its own section above).

### Data integrity

`normalizeData()` (called from `load()`, from every merge/import/file-sync entry point, and, for tests, from the `resetState()` glue) is the single source of truth for schema defaults: workstream `id`/`color`/`order` backfilled and the array re-sorted by `order`; an item's `id`/`name`/`owner`/`status`/`updatedAt`/`itStatus`/`businessStatus`/`budgetStatus` backfilled, `order` backfilled from current array position per workstream (a separate pass, after `workstreamId` reassignment below so it's never keyed by a stale one — see "Manual item order and drag-and-drop reordering"), an orphaned/stale `workstreamId` (pointing at a workstream that no longer exists) reset to `null` — Unassigned, same as an item deliberately created that way (see "Quick-add scope items, the Unassigned bucket, and the Pending category" below) — rather than an arbitrary real workstream, so a broken reference surfaces for triage instead of silently landing wherever `workstreams[0]` happens to be; an orphaned/missing `categoryId` reassigned to the first category, a missing `startDate` defaulted from `dueDate`, a non-string `actualDate` defaulted to `null`, a missing `milestones` defaulted to `[]`, and each milestone's own `id`/`name`/`status`/`actualDate`/`notApplicable` backfilled (`false` for `notApplicable` — see "Not Applicable milestones" below) and a missing `dueDate` defaulted from its *parent item's* `dueDate` — then, once milestones are backfilled, any item with `milestones.length > 0` has its `status` recomputed via `computedStatusFromMilestones()` regardless of whatever value was saved (see "Item status roll-up"). A stale `filterWorkstreamId` (the one selector shared by Planning/Review/Dashboard — see "The shared workstream selector" above) is cleared if it no longer resolves, a stale/invalid `mode` falls back to `'planning'` (checked against the `MODES` array), and — since Review mode requires a specific workstream — `mode` additionally falls back to `'planning'` if it's `'review'` with no `filterWorkstreamId` selected. `deletedWorkstreamIds`/`deletedItemIds` are coerced to arrays and filtered to well-formed `{id, ...}` entries; `categories` and `reviewCycles` get the same array-coercion, and every category's own `id`/`name`/`milestones`/`order` is backfilled the same way a workstream's is. **If `categories` ends up empty** (a pre-categories save, or every category somehow got deleted), `normalizeData()` reseeds the whole out-of-the-box set — `DEFAULT_CATEGORIES` (see "Quick-add scope items, the Unassigned bucket, and the Pending category" below for where that constant comes from) — this is also how a pre-categories item picks up a valid `categoryId` on first load after the upgrade, since the orphaned-`categoryId` backfill runs after categories are guaranteed non-empty. Review cycles get `id`/`startedAt`/`completedAt`/`cancelledAt`/`confirmations`/`milestoneConfirmations` backfilled but are otherwise left alone — there's no cross-referential repair needed since a cycle referencing a since-deleted workstream is simply unreachable through the shared sidebar (which only lists live workstreams), not actively cleaned up; see "Review mode" below for why that's the deliberate choice rather than an oversight.

It also runs a one-time schema migration: a pre-nested-milestones save could have a top-level item with `type: 'milestone'` (milestones used to be their own top-level item, siblings of scope items, before this became a nested checklist). Rather than silently dropping these on first load under the new schema, `normalizeData()` folds each one into its own new single-milestone item (`startDate`/`dueDate` both set to the old milestone's `dueDate`, `milestones: [{ ...that one milestone }]`) and strips the legacy rows out of `items`. `type` itself is `delete`d off every item as part of this pass, since it no longer means anything under the new schema.

**Every place that assigns `workstreams`/`items` from an external source calls `normalizeData()`, not just `load()`** — the current call sites are `load()`, both branches of `applyImport()`, `restoreBackup()`, `linkFile()`'s merge-on-first-link branch, `reconnectFile()`, and `initFileSync()`. If you add another data-loading path (e.g. a new sync mechanism), route it through `normalizeData()` too, or you'll reintroduce the exact bug class this fixed — a render function assuming a field exists that an older/hand-edited/externally-sourced save doesn't have.

`normalizeData()` is also the security boundary for ids: every workstream/item/milestone id is interpolated unescaped into `onclick` HTML attributes at render time (`esc()` only covers *content* fields — name, owner — never `id`). That's fine for locally-created data since `genId()` only ever produces plain alphanumeric strings, but `mergeData()` and `applyImport('replace')` trust ids from an external `.json` file as-is. `isSafeId(id)` (`/^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]+$/`) plus the per-entity checks in `normalizeData()`'s three loops (workstreams, items, milestones) regenerate anything that couldn't have come from `genId()`, closing that off at the one place every external-data path already routes through — see `tests/cases/sync.test.js`'s "regenerates a workstream/item/milestone id" tests.

## Data durability

Pulse now matches PromptLab/mytasks' full data-durability feature set: JSON export/import, rolling local backups, deletion tombstones with toast undo, and optional File System Access API file sync. The "Data" dropdown in the topbar (`toggleDataMenu()`/`closeDataMenu()`, closed by the document-level click listener when a click lands outside `#dataDropdown`) is the entry point for Export, Import, and Backups. File sync deliberately has no entry there — see "File System Access API sync" below for why it lives entirely in the topbar's `#fileSyncIndicator` instead.

### Toast with undo

`showToast(msg, isError, undoAction)` — the third argument is new. When present, the toast shows an "Undo" button (`#toastUndoBtn`) and stays up 6s instead of 2s; `triggerToastUndo()` (wired to the button's `onclick`) invokes it and dismisses the toast immediately. Only one undo action is live at a time (`toastUndoAction`, a single module-level slot) — showing any other toast overwrites it, matching the single visible toast.

### Confirm modal — replaces native `confirm()`

`openConfirmModal({title, body, action, confirmLabel})` / `confirmModalAction()` / `closeConfirmModal()` replace the plain `window.confirm()` dialogs earlier versions used for delete. `modalTarget = {action}` holds the pending callback; clicking the modal's action button (`#confirmModalActionBtn`) runs it and closes the modal. This exists specifically so confirmations can describe *what* will happen (e.g. cascade counts) and hand off into the undo-toast flow below, which a same-tick `confirm()` return value can't do.

**`confirmLabel` defaults to `"Delete"`** — this one shared modal backs every confirm-then-act flow in the app, not just deletes (cancelling a review cycle, restoring a backup, applying a category's milestone-template sync to its items), and for a while those non-delete callers didn't override it, so the button read "Delete" for an action that wasn't a delete at all — confusing enough that a user reported a category-sync as "not working" when they'd likely declined it via a button that looked like it would destroy something. Every caller whose action isn't literally a delete now passes an explicit `confirmLabel` (`'Cancel Review'`, `'Restore'`, `'Apply Changes'`) — `tests/cases/*.test.js` assert the label alongside each of those flows specifically to keep this from silently regressing back to "Delete" if a new confirm-gated action gets added without one.

### Deletion tombstones and undo

`deletedWorkstreamIds`/`deletedItemIds` (each `{id, deletedAt}[]`) record "this id used to exist and was intentionally removed" — without them, an id absent from `workstreams`/`items` is indistinguishable from an id a merge has simply never seen, and a stale copy from an old linked file or import could silently resurrect something the user deleted on purpose. `tombstone(list, id)`/`untombstone(list, id)` add/remove an entry.

`deleteItem(id)` opens the confirm modal; its `action` splices the item out, tombstones it, then calls `showToast('Item deleted', false, undoFn)` where `undoFn` untombstones it and splices it back to its original index. The undo callback must clear the tombstone(s) it set, or a delete-then-undo would leave the item permanently un-mergeable from any linked file/import that still has it.

**`deleteWorkstreamFromModal()` no longer cascades to that workstream's own items — an explicit user request** ("if i remove a workstream that has scope items, move them to unassigned"), reversing an earlier design that deleted them right along with the workstream (tombstoned, undoable only as one bundled action). Losing real work — milestones, tags, dates, review history — just because the workstream containing it was removed was the actual complaint, not the workstream deletion itself. Its confirm-modal `action` now only removes the workstream itself, and separately walks `items` setting `workstreamId = null` (plus a fresh `updatedAt`, so the change is real for merge purposes — see "Merge and last-write-wins" below) on every item that belonged to it, collecting their ids into `movedItemIds` as it goes; nothing about a moved item is otherwise touched — it keeps its own category, milestones, tags, dates. This lands a moved item in exactly the same "Unassigned" bucket a stale/orphaned `workstreamId` already gets from `normalizeData()` (see "Quick-add scope items, the Unassigned bucket..." below) — `unassignedItemsSorted()`/`unassignedSectionHtml()` are keyed purely on `workstreamId == null`, with no assumption that everything landing there came through the quick-add flow's Pending category, so a moved item with a real, already-assigned category shows up there normally: fully editable (its Category select isn't locked — only a genuinely Pending item's is, see "A still-Pending item's category can never be changed..." above), Actual date and Dependency still visible, and reachable to re-assign to a real workstream again at any time through the ordinary item modal. The confirm dialog's own body text changed to match ("N items will be moved to Unassigned") — no longer describing a deletion that no longer happens.

The undo callback restores the workstream (spliced back to its original index) and then reassigns *only* the items this specific delete moved, by id (`movedItemIds`) — not a whole-`items`-array snapshot the way the old cascade-delete version used, and deliberately guarded on `it.workstreamId === null` before restoring: if one of the moved items was independently reassigned to a different real workstream in the few seconds before Undo was clicked, undo must not clobber that later, unrelated edit back to the deleted workstream. There's nothing left to tombstone/untombstone on the item side at all any more — only the workstream id itself still goes through `tombstone(deletedWorkstreamIds, wsId)`/`untombstone(...)`, since items are never removed from `items` in the first place.

**`deleteItem(id)` allows deleting a still-Pending (Unassigned) item, same as any other** — an explicit user request. An earlier version of this function refused outright (a toast, the confirm modal never even opening) for a Pending-category item, on the reasoning that one still waiting on real triage (see "Quick-add scope items, the Unassigned bucket, and the Pending category" below) shouldn't be able to just disappear before that triage happens; a later, separate user request reversed that, so a not-yet-triaged item now goes through the exact same confirm-modal-and-undo-toast flow as everything else. Both places `deleteItem()` is reachable from — the row's own trash icon (`itemRowHtml()`'s `.item-actions`) and the full edit modal's Delete button (`deleteItemFromModal()`) — funnel through this one function, so this applies to both without a second check anywhere else.

### Merge and last-write-wins

`mergeData(data, opts)` is the additive merge used by every "read data from outside `localStorage`" path (`linkFile()`, `reconnectFile()`, `initFileSync()`, `doFileSyncWrite()`'s cross-tab-conflict branch, `pollFileSync()`, and `applyImport('merge')`). It merges incoming tombstones first (union by id, keeping the later `deletedAt`, covering `deletedWorkstreamIds`/`deletedItemIds`/`deletedMilestoneIds`/`deletedActionLogIds`/`deletedDecisionLogIds`), then workstreams *and* categories (workstreams merge field-by-field into an already-known id via `mergeWorkstreamFields()` — see below — categories stay simple additive-by-id-or-name, merged silently either way, not counted in the return value, mirroring how mytasks' projects merge), then review cycles (field-by-field into an already-known id via `mergeReviewCycle()` — see below — additive-by-id for a genuinely new one), then items. Returns `{added, updated, conflicts, changed}` — `added`/`updated` only ever count items (unchanged from before); `conflicts` is covered under "Conflict detection" below; `changed` is the umbrella "did this merge actually mutate anything at all" flag `doFileSyncWrite()`/`pollFileSync()` gate their own `save()`/`render()` on (see their own paragraph further down) — `added > 0 || updated > 0` alone isn't enough any more, since a merge can now be entirely about review-cycle confirmations or a workstream's `actionLog`/`decisionLog`, with zero item changes.

**Review cycles and workstreams used to merge as one opaque, whole unit each — `mergeData()` simply skipped an incoming one once its id was already known locally, silently discarding everything on it.** This was a real, user-reported gap, surfaced by a direct question about what happens when several people run a review in parallel against the same file synced through something like SharePoint/OneDrive: three reviewers confirming different milestones in the same active cycle, or attaching meeting-minutes action items to the same workstream around the same time, would each write their own full local copy of the cycle/workstream back to the file — and since the *other* side's id was already known, its confirmations/`changeLog`/`actionLog`/`decisionLog` entries were never merged in at all, just silently overwritten by whichever write reached the file last. Items/milestones never had this problem (see the per-field milestone merge just below, which predates this) — review cycles and workstreams now get the identical treatment, one level up.

**`mergeKeyedRecords(existingList, incomingList, keyField, tombstones, respectTombstones)`** is the shared primitive both new merges are built on — the same "newer `updatedAt` wins, an id not yet seen locally gets appended" idiom `mergeMilestonesArray()` already used for an item's own milestones, generalized to any flat array of `{[keyField]: id, updatedAt, ...}` records: a review cycle's own `confirmations`/`milestoneConfirmations`, or a workstream's `actionLog`/`decisionLog`. `tombstones`/`respectTombstones` are omitted for the two confirmation arrays — a confirmation record is never actually removed (see "Confirmation is milestone-level" above), only flipped, so there's nothing to tombstone there — but passed for `actionLog`/`decisionLog`, which *can* be genuinely deleted (see below).

**`mergeReviewCycle(existing, incoming)`** merges each piece of a cycle independently: `confirmations`/`milestoneConfirmations` via `mergeKeyedRecords()`; `changeLog` as a plain union by its own `id` (append-only and never mutated after creation, so no `updatedAt` comparison needed — just keep every entry either side has, re-sorted by `changedAt` so the merged history still reads chronologically regardless of which side logged which entry); `minutes` as one whole nested object, newer `updatedAt` wins (the same coarser granularity an item's own *scalar* fields get, not milestones' finer per-field treatment — minutes are normally written by one person after a meeting, so a real per-field merge of its own `actionItems` would mean a second `mergeMilestonesArray()`-shaped function for comparatively little real-world benefit); `completedAt`/`cancelledAt` (plus the count snapshots that travel with `completedAt`) are set once, then frozen, so merging is just "prefer whichever side actually has it set."

**Two people clicking "Start review cycle" for the same workstream before either side had synced the other's write leaves two distinct cycles both marked active** — `activeReviewCycle()` only ever finds the first match, so the second would otherwise sit invisibly in `reviewCycles` with its own confirmations/`changeLog` nobody ever sees again. `reconcileDuplicateActiveCycles()`, called from `normalizeData()` (so it runs on every load/merge, not just right after a merge that could have caused it — cheap, and self-heals the same situation however it arose, including from data saved before this reconciliation existed at all), folds every later-started active cycle for a workstream into the earliest one via `mergeReviewCycle()` itself, then drops the duplicate.

**`mergeWorkstreamFields(existing, incoming, respectTombstones)`** is `mergeReviewCycle()`'s counterpart one level up: `actionLog`/`decisionLog` merge per-entry via `mergeKeyedRecords()`, tombstone-aware (`deletedActionLogIds`/`deletedDecisionLogIds` — new tombstone lists, same shape as `deletedItemIds`); `name`/`color` merge as plain scalars, newer `updatedAt` wins.
